{"chunk_id":"fc3022","wall_time_seconds":0.039351265,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":4617,"output":"# Select sufficient checks\n\nRead the accepted contract and requested work boundary before selecting commands. Inspect the actual diff or relevant implementation, declared tooling, and tests. A task's expected edit scope helps locate effects; it does not excuse ignoring dependent behavior affected by its changes.\n\n## Scope and evidence\n\n| Boundary | Evidence to establish |\n| --- | --- |\n| Task | Its acceptance conditions and directly affected behavior, including necessary regressions. |\n| Selected change | Changed contracts, affected consumers, compatibility, and integration paths. |\n| Milestone or phase | Separate implementation code review and check evidence, applicable PLAN exits, constituent results, cross-component behavior and report/TODO evidence. Load boundary review for explicit review tasks. |\n| Project | Requested acceptance, integration, build, packaging, or other project-level checks. |\n\nFor feature work, use the owning FEATURE-TASKS and relevant feature contracts alongside applicable main requirements. A feature's scoped parent checkbox does not establish the whole-project milestone or phase exit conditions.\n\n## Select checks\n\n1. Identify observable acceptance conditions and the evidence each needs. Reuse meaningful tests, inspections, examples, or measurements; do not create artificial tests merely to populate a checklist.\n2. Select direct checks for affected behavior, dependent checks for relevant consumers, integration checks for collaborating components, and exit checks for the requested boundary. Include failure paths and operational risks where the contract calls for them.\n3. Follow mandatory project commands, suites, supported environments, and boundary checks. Run the declared full suite when project policy or the requested scope requires it; report why checks are omitted or unavailable. An apparently small change does not waive governing requirements.\n4. Use existing test routing when helpful, but inspect whether it still reflects the changed components and contracts. Report missing or stale mapping entries rather than treating the map as proof of coverage.\n5. Identify coverage gaps and unsuitable checks. Return needed test design or changes through the implementation workflow to **sdd-tdd**; verification does not silently add tests or redefine acceptance.\n\nSelect checks proportionate to the requested claim. For example, compilation does not establish runtime behavior, a unit check may not establish packaging, and a timing run does not establish correctness. Documentation may need inspected contracts, executable examples, link checks, or documentation generation instead of unrelated runtime tests. Performance or security claims require evidence appropriate to the particular claim.\n# Execution and evidence\n\n## Run against an identified state\n\n- Record the branch and commit, relevant pending changes, command working directory, and material runtime or dependency conditions. Evidence from committed HEAD alone does not describe uncommitted code being tested.\n- Use the project's declared commands and environment. Inspect potentially mutating build, test, or example commands before execution; use controlled fixtures or temporary output locations where practical. Do not install dependencies, change configuration, or operate external services beyond the authorized scope.\n- Capture the actual command, exit status, relevant output, counts, warnings, skips, and artifacts where applicable. Inspect collection and execution: zero selected tests or unexpected deselection does not verify the intended behavior merely because the runner exits successfully.\n- Run independent checks together only when they do not contend for shared resources or invalidate each other's results. Keep dependent checks in the required order.\n- If execution is interrupted, report the incomplete run and available output. Do not describe a started command as a completed check.\n\n## Assess coverage\n\n| Condition state | Meaning |\n| --- | --- |\n| Verified | Observed evidence supports this condition for the stated implementation and environment. |\n| Failed | Observed evidence contradicts the condition or its required check failed; identify the cause separately where known. |\n| Blocked | Required evidence cannot be obtained because of a concrete prerequisite or execution problem. |\n| Not checked | The condition was not assessed; give the reason and remaining work. |\n\nAssociate conditions with the checks that actually exercise them. Record partial coverage, expected failures, skipped scenarios, platform limitations, or manual inspection explicitly. A passing suite is evidence about its tested behavior, not proof that every requirement is covered. Do not invent coverage percentages or measurements.\n\nReuse prior evidence only when its implementation state, environment, and condition coverage are still applicable; identify it as prior evidence. After relevant code, test, configuration, dependency, or generated-output changes, repeat affected checks. Do not repeatedly run already sufficient checks without a changed state, failure, or unresolved gap that justifies it.\n\n## Preserve and return evidence\n\nInspect resulting worktree changes to distinguish expected generated output from unexpected mutations. Preserve unrelated work and report unexplained changes; do not reset or clean the repository to conceal them. Temporary fixtures owned by this verification operation may be cleaned up when no longer needed.\n\nReturn evidence facts and locations to the active implementation workflow (**sdd-implement** or **sdd-steer**) and **sdd-report**, including unresolved failures and limitations. Preserve raw outputs or artifacts when the project requires them or they are needed to review a consequential claim. Honor the project-designated evidence location. If none is designated, return concise durable facts for the active owner to record beside the owning TASKS or FEATURE-TASKS entry, or in that entry's existing linked evidence. For taskless maintenance, use the ordinary change report or commit body rather than inventing a task ID or evidence file. Verification returns facts; the active owner persists them with its scoped result.\n\n## Durable evidence facts\n\n| Fact | Record |\n| --- | --- |\n| Identity and state | Owning task/change, branch, tested commit or pending diff, authoritative condition. |\n| Check | Command, relevant environment, observed exit/outcome, material counts, warnings/skips. |\n| Coverage | Conditions actually exercised, gaps, blocked checks, and evidence location when separate. |\n| Prior evidence | Historical source and applicability; do not present it as a fresh run. |\n| Test-first exception | Concrete limitation, applicable policy or user authorization, alternative evidence actually obtained, and remaining gap. |\n\nCarry these facts to **sdd-report** without requiring a new JSON schema or journal. Preserve raw output when needed to review a consequential claim. For branch integration, identify the working tip and target tip tested in the prospective merge, and retain merged-state outcomes in existing boundary evidence or the merge commit body.\n# Issue, commit, and PR drafts\n\nKeep the same task identity across all outputs. Use the project-wide ID from its owning TASKS or FEATURE-TASKS entry; include a resolved issue reference only when **sdd-forge** supplies a unique repository and number. Match the requested object's time perspective: an issue describes intended work, while a commit or PR describes actual changes and available evidence.\n\n## Task issue\n\nReturn `title` and `body` separately. Format the title as `[<task-id>] <task title>`, using the owning task list. Draft the body from the task outcome, reason and project context, expected scope and dependencies, objective acceptance and prescribed checks, and source document links. Add relevant kind-specific details. For example:\n\n- **Performance:** Describe the problem with the current implementation, explain how the proposed approach may improve it, and identify the measurement plan and any established baseline. Do not present an expected speedup as an achieved result.\n- **Security:** State the affected guarantee and risk without exposing exploit instructions or credentials.\n\n**sdd-report** owns the title and body format. Preserve an exact task identity marker when one is supplied. Never invent host labels, milestone associations, issue URLs, or resolution state.\n\n## Git commit\n\nFor a first SDD commit or adoption backfill, load **sdd-manage**'s [repository bootstrap](../../sdd-manage/references/repository-bootstrap.md). Confirm the commit owner has included or validly retained root disclosure/usage records and README links within scope. Return missing bootstrap evidence to that owner; drafting does not perform bootstrap or establish a committed result.\n\nDraft a short imperative subject naming the actual change. For task-associated work, always include the owning task ID. For preparation or maintenance without an assigned task, do not invent an ID. Use a body when the reason, verification, migration implications, or multiple issue references need explanation. Base it on the inspected diff and checks, not merely the task brief.\n\nUse `Refs owner/repo#123` when the commit advances an issue without completing it. Use `Fixes owner/repo#123`, `Resolves owner/repo#123`, or `Closes owner/repo#123` when the commit fully resolves that issue and the evidence supports completion. A commit may reference multiple issues, with a separate appropriate reference for each; omit issue references when no verified association exists. On GitHub, closing keywords may close an issue when the commit reaches the default branch. **sdd-forge** still reconciles issue closure after verified task completion, without waiting for that automation. If verification has not been run, say so in a proposed body rather than claiming it passed. The active implementation workflow makes and checks its commits; **sdd-manage** coordinates persistence for other authorized repository changes.\n\n## Merge commit\n\nDraft a subject identifying the actual feature, steering amendment, or selected range, such as `Merge phase 2 Archive support` or `Merge feature ZIP support`. A main milestone/task subset does not justify a merge draft while its phase remains incomplete. Return the subject and body separately. Include the working and target branches, starting checkpoint and verified parent tips, included task IDs where applicable, boundary and merged-state checks, material conflict resolutions, and limitations. Do not represent an amendment as completion of the next task. The coordinator performs the explicit two-parent merge; drafting the message does not authorize extra work or a hosted PR.\n\n## Pull request\n\nDraft a title and description only when requested; this skill does not create a PR. Scope the text to the actual branch diff and its included task IDs. Summarize **What**, **Why**, **Verification**, and **Result**; add the relevant fields from [change kinds](change-kinds.md). State the base branch and integration status only when known. List unrun checks, limitations, and remaining work explicitly rather than presenting partial work as complete.\n\nA code health PR should explain the ownership or maintainability problem and the checks supporting behavior preservation. A performance PR should include baseline and current times, input size, environment, method, and whether the measured gain is meaningful. Do not imply that GitHub PR operations are available through **sdd-forge**.\n\n## Examples\n\nThese show formatting for an issue title, commit message, and PR draft. Use actual task IDs, issue references, checks, and results for the current work.\n\n### Issue title\n\n```text\n[T-012] Implement ZIP stream support\n```\n\n### Commit message\n\n```text\nClarify filesystem utility ownership (T-041)\n\nMove shared filesystem helpers into the module named by the project layout.\nUpdate its documented responsibility; the focused unit suite passed.\n\nRefs owner/repo#123\n```\n\n### Code health PR\n\n```markdown\n# 🧹 Clarify filesystem utility ownership\n\n- 🎯 **What:** Renamed `common.py` to `fs.py` and updated its documented responsibility.\n- 💡 **Why:** A focused module name makes ownership clear under the project's layout rules.\n- ✅ **Verification:** Inspected layout references and passed the focused unit tests.\n- ✨ **Result:** Filesystem utility ownership is explicit, with behavior preservation supported by the cited checks.\n```\n# In-code documentation\n\nInspect the selected modules and their callers, tests, and accepted contracts before writing. Documentation must explain the code that exists without presenting an implementation discrepancy as a revised requirement.\n\n## Select a style\n\n1. Use the style specified by project instructions or documentation policy, including language-specific rules and tooling requirements.\n2. If no style is specified, use an established professional convention suitable for the language and documentation generator. Google-style docstrings are an appropriate Python default; other languages use their established documentation comments or equivalent.\n3. Apply the selected style consistently within the edited scope. Preserve compatible existing conventions and report conflicting styles or requirements rather than converting unrelated modules.\n\n## Module and API coverage\n\n| Element | Documentation to assess |\n| --- | --- |\n| Every project-owned code module in scope | Purpose, canonical responsibility, and relevant usage, constraints, or collaborators. Use a module docstring or the language's equivalent. |\n| Public classes and interfaces | Responsibility, contracts, invariants, lifecycle, and important public attributes. |\n| Public functions and methods | Behavior, parameters, return values, exceptions, side effects, and relevant preconditions or guarantees. |\n| Consequential internal interfaces | Non-obvious contracts, assumptions, ordering, ownership, and failure behavior needed by maintainers. |\n| Implementation comments | Reasons for consequential decisions, workarounds, or constraints that the code alone does not explain. |\n\nInclude applicable details rather than filling every possible section. For example, document resource ownership, concurrency, cancellation, units, encoding, or persistence semantics when those affect correct use. A small module may need only a precise responsibility statement; a complex API may need examples and operational constraints. Avoid narrating obvious statements, repeating signatures without explanation, or asserting guarantees that the code and evidence do not support.\n\n## Maintain and check\n\n- Review affected module and API documentation after substantive code changes, including changed signatures, errors, defaults, ownership, and side effects.\n- Check names and contracts against inspected implementation, callers, tests, and accepted requirements. Report requirement conflicts to the user under the skill's amendment protocol.\n- Use declared documentation lint, syntax, generation, or example checks when relevant and available. Report unrun checks and distinguish inspection from execution.\n- Inspect the final diff for unintended behavior changes. Preserve annotations, executable statements, and public signatures; route necessary code repairs to the implementation workflow.\n# Execute one task\n\nRead its outcome, dependencies, acceptance conditions, expected paths, and relevant SPEC, design, PLAN, and layout. For feature work, include applicable accepted feature documents. Inspect existing implementation and tests before editing; preserve useful pending work and project conventions.\n\n## Coordinate the development cycle\n\n1. Use **sdd-tdd** to derive relevant scenarios and write or revise tests. For changed behavior or a bug fix, observe the intended failure before the production change; apply authorized exceptions and preserve existing work when historical RED evidence is absent. Behavior-preserving refactoring can use established coverage.\n2. Apply production changes within the task scope. Satisfy the accepted contract rather than merely weakening an assertion or hardcoding a passing fixture. Keep component ownership and physical placement consistent with governing design and layout.\n3. Use **sdd-docs** after substantive changes to review affected module and API documentation, README, guides, and examples. It reports amendments needed in governing documents to the user; independent work may continue, but a disputed acceptance condition remains unresolved.\n4. Use **sdd-verify** for the required checks and acceptance assessment. Inspect actual evidence, failures, warnings, skips, coverage limitations, and declared project or boundary checks.\n5. Repair implementation failures within scope. Coordinate test repairs with **sdd-tdd** and documentation repairs with **sdd-docs**. Repeat affected verification after relevant changes; existing evidence may be reused only when still applicable.\n\nDevelopment-cycle test runs do not replace the verification campaign. Verification does not mark the task complete or repair code itself. Read the evidence and decide completion under [completion and checkpoints](completion-and-checkpoints.md).\n\n## Scope and blockers\n\n- Preserve unrelated code, documentation, and pending changes. Do not reset or delete existing implementation to reconstruct a test-first history.\n- Report missing requirements, conflicting authoritative documents, unavailable prerequisites, or dependencies outside the selected range. Do not turn an unresolved decision into implementation policy.\n- Report observed pre-existing or unrelated failures; do not silently expand repairs beyond authorization or claim the suite passes. Resolve whether required acceptance can be established before marking completion.\n- If work cannot be completed, keep the task unchecked or correct an unsupported checked claim, including one from an earlier durable task result. Preserve partial work, historical evidence, and any unresolved completion-reassessment note; report the blocker and remaining steps. Commit a partial checkpoint only when the user or project policy authorizes it, clearly distinguishing it from a completed task commit.\n- Do not create feature overlays, reconcile the task hierarchy, or perform a checkpoint amendment merely to make the selected task pass. Those changes require their own established scope and owning workflow.\n"}
{"chunk_id":"7ec275","wall_time_seconds":0.000030056,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":1772,"output":"\"\"\"Pure whole-input counting and its immutable statistics value.\"\"\"\n\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\nimport re\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass TextStats:\n    \"\"\"Immutable nonnegative integer line and word counts.\n\n    Args:\n        lines: Number of CRLF, CR, or LF terminated lines and final segments.\n        words: Number of whitespace-separated words.\n\n    Raises:\n        TypeError: A count is not an integer (booleans are rejected).\n        ValueError: A count is negative.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    lines: int\n    words: int\n\n    def __post_init__(self) -> None:\n        for field in (\"lines\", \"words\"):\n            value = getattr(self, field)\n            if type(value) is not int:\n                raise TypeError(f\"{field} must be an integer\")\n            if value < 0:\n                raise ValueError(f\"{field} must be nonnegative\")\n\n\ndef count_text(text: str, *, strip_bom: bool = True) -> TextStats:\n    \"\"\"Count a string without modifying it or emitting output.\n\n    Args:\n        text: The complete decoded input.\n        strip_bom: Remove exactly one leading U+FEFF when true. Interior and\n            subsequent BOMs remain ordinary non-whitespace characters.\n\n    Returns:\n        Immutable counts. Only CRLF, lone CR, and LF terminate lines; a\n        nonempty final unterminated segment adds one line. Empty text has no\n        lines, and trailing terminators add no phantom line. Words follow\n        Python's Unicode whitespace splitting rules.\n    \"\"\"\n    text = _normalize_text(text, strip_bom=strip_bom)\n    # Normalizing CRLF first prevents its two characters counting twice.\n    normalized = text.replace(\"\\r\\n\", \"\\n\").replace(\"\\r\", \"\\n\")\n    lines = normalized.count(\"\\n\")\n    if normalized and not normalized.endswith(\"\\n\"):\n        lines += 1\n    return TextStats(lines, len(text.split()))\n\n\ndef _normalize_text(text: str, *, strip_bom: bool = True) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Apply the whole decoded input's BOM policy exactly once.\"\"\"\n    return text[1:] if strip_bom and text.startswith(\"\\ufeff\") else text\n\n\ndef _select_lines(text: str, start: int, end: int) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Select inclusive logical lines from already normalized decoded text.\n\n    Preserve contents and CRLF/CR/LF terminators. Unicode separators are\n    contents, and a trailing terminator does not create a phantom line.\n    \"\"\"\n    selected = []\n    offset = 0\n    number = 1\n    for match in re.finditer(r\"\\r\\n|\\r|\\n\", text):\n        if start <= number <= end:\n            selected.append(text[offset:match.end()])\n        offset = match.end()\n        number += 1\n        if number > end:\n            return \"\".join(selected)\n    if offset < len(text) and start <= number <= end:\n        selected.append(text[offset:])\n    return \"\".join(selected)\n\"\"\"Acquire complete named-file bytes without newline translation.\"\"\"\n\nimport os\n\nfrom .core import TextStats, count_text\n\n\ndef count_file(path: str | os.PathLike[str], *, strip_bom: bool = True) -> TextStats:\n    \"\"\"Count a UTF-8 file silently, leaving its bytes unchanged.\n\n    Args:\n        path: String or filesystem path to the named input.\n        strip_bom: Remove exactly one leading BOM before counting.\n\n    Returns:\n        Immutable line/word counts using count_text's CR/LF and Unicode rules.\n\n    Raises:\n        OSError: The file cannot be opened or read.\n        UnicodeDecodeError: Complete input is not valid UTF-8.\n\n    The owned binary handle closes before counting; no caller stream is owned.\n    \"\"\"\n    return count_text(_read_text(path), strip_bom=strip_bom)\n\n\ndef _read_text(path: str | os.PathLike[str]) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Decode complete named-file bytes strictly, closing the owned handle.\"\"\"\n    with open(path, \"rb\") as source:\n        return source.read().decode(\"utf-8\")\n\"\"\"Public interfaces for whole-input text statistics.\"\"\"\n\nfrom .core import TextStats, count_text\nfrom .io import count_file\n\n__all__ = [\"TextStats\", \"count_text\", \"count_file\"]\n\"\"\"Expose the named-file command through python -m textstats.\"\"\"\n\nfrom .cli import main\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    raise SystemExit(main())\nPYTHON ?= python\nDIST_DIR ?= dist\n\n.PHONY: dist check\n\n# Explicit package/test files keep bytecode caches and workflow resources out.\ndist:\n\tmkdir -p \"$(DIST_DIR)\"\n\t$(PYTHON) -m tarfile -c \"$(DIST_DIR)/textstats.tar.gz\" textstats/*.py README.md Makefile AI_DISCLOSURE.md SDD-MANAGER.md docs tests/__init__.py tests/unit/*.py tests/integration/*.py\n\ncheck:\n\tPYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 $(PYTHON) -m unittest discover -s tests/unit -t . -v\n\tPYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 $(PYTHON) -m unittest discover -s tests/integration -t . count_text\n\n\ndef _parse_range(value: str) -> tuple[str, str]:\n    \"\"\"Validate unbounded ASCII decimals without integer-string conversion.\"\"\"\n    parts = value.split(\":\")\n    if len(parts) != 2 or any(not p or any(c < \"0\" or c > \"9\" for c in p) for p in parts):\n        raise argparse.ArgumentTypeError(\"lines must be positive ASCII START:END\")\n    start, end = (p.lstrip(\"0\") for p in parts)\n    if not start or not end or (len(start), start) > (len(end), end):\n        raise argparse.ArgumentTypeError(\"lines require 1 <= START <= END\")\n    return start, end\n\n\nclass _SingleRange(argparse.Action):\n    \"\"\"Reject repeated options rather than accepting argparse's last value.\"\"\"\n    def __call__(self, parser, namespace, values, option_string=None):\n        if getattr(namespace, self.dest) is not None:\n            parser.error(\"--lines may be specified only once\")\n        setattr(namespace, self.dest, values)\n\n\ndef _available_endpoint(value: str, text_length: int) -> int:\n    \"\"\"Clamp only to an impossible line past EOF before safe conversion.\"\"\"\n    past_eof = text_length + 1\n    limit = str(past_eof)\n    return past_eof if (len(value), value) > (len(limit), limit) else int(value)\n\n\ndef main(argv: Sequence[str] | None = None) -> int:\n    \"\"\"Run the named-file/stdin CLI; argparse exits for help or invalid usage.\n\n    Args:\n        argv: Explicit arguments, or None to use process arguments.\n\n    Returns:\n        Zero after emitting exactly one line of successful text counts; one\n        for source read/decode failures, with a diagnostic on stderr.\n\n    Validation precedes acquisition. Expected OSError and UnicodeDecodeError\n    failures identify the input without emitting partial counts or a traceback.\n    \"\"\"\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(prog=\"textstats\", allow_abbrev=False,\n                                     description=\"Count lines and words in UTF-8 files or stdin.\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"--keep-bom\", action=\"store_true\", help=\"retain a leading BOM\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"--lines\", type=_parse_range, action=_SingleRange,\n                        metavar=\"START:END\", help=\"select inclusive logical lines\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"input\", metavar=\"INPUT\", help=\"named UTF-8 input file, or - for stdin\")\n    args = parser.parse_args(argv)\n    try:\n        if args.input != \"-\" and args.lines is None:\n            stats = count_file(args.input, strip_bom=not args.keep_bom)\n        else:\n            # stdin is borrowed: read its binary stream without a context manager.\n            decoded = sys.stdin.buffer.read().decode(\"utf-8\") if args.input == \"-\" else _read_text(args.input)\n            text = _normalize_text(decoded, strip_bom=not args.keep_bom)\n            if args.lines is not None:\n                start, end = (_available_endpoint(value, len(text)) for value in args.lines)\n                text = _select_lines(text, start, end)\n            stats = count_text(text, strip_bom=False)\n    except (OSError, UnicodeDecodeError) as error:\n        source = \"stdin\" if args.input == \"-\" else repr(args.input)\n        print(f\"textstats: {source}: {error}\", file=sys.stderr)\n        return 1\n    print(f\"lines={stats.lines} words={stats.words}\")\n    return 0\n\"\"\"Pure whole-input counting and its immutable statistics value.\"\"\"\n\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\nimport re\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass TextStats:\n    \"\"\"Immutable nonnegative integer line and word counts.\n\n    Args:\n        lines: Number of CRLF, CR, or LF terminated lines and final segments.\n        words: Number of whitespace-separated words.\n\n    Raises:\n        TypeError: A count is not an integer (booleans are rejected).\n        ValueError: A count is negative.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    lines: int\n    words: int\n\n    def __post_init__(self) -> None:\n        for field in (\"lines\", \"words\"):\n            value = getattr(self, field)\n            if type(value) is not int:\n                raise TypeError(f\"{field} must be an integer\")\n            if value < 0:\n                raise ValueError(f\"{field} must be nonnegative\")\n\n\ndef count_text(text: str, *, strip_bom: bool = True) -> TextStats:\n    \"\"\"Count a string without modifying it or emitting output.\n\n    Args:\n        text: The complete decoded input.\n        strip_bom: Remove exactly one leading U+FEFF when true. Interior and\n            subsequent BOMs remain ordinary non-whitespace characters.\n\n    Returns:\n        Immutable counts. Only CRLF, lone CR, and LF terminate lines; a\n        nonempty final unterminated segment adds one line. Empty text has no\n        lines, and trailing terminators add no phantom line. Words follow\n        Python's Unicode whitespace splitting rules.\n    \"\"\"\n    text = _normalize_text(text, strip_bom=strip_bom)\n    # Normalizing CRLF first prevents its two characters counting twice.\n    normalized = text.replace(\"\\r\\n\", \"\\n\").replace(\"\\r\", \"\\n\")\n    lines = normalized.count(\"\\n\")\n    if normalized and not normalized.endswith(\"\\n\"):\n        lines += 1\n    return TextStats(lines, len(text.split()))\n\n\ndef _normalize_text(text: str, *, strip_bom: bool = True) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Apply the whole decoded input's BOM policy exactly once.\"\"\"\n    return text[1:] if strip_bom and text.startswith(\"\\ufeff\") else text\n\n\ndef _select_lines(text: str, start: int, end: int) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Select inclusive logical lines from already normalized decoded text.\n\n    Preserve contents and CRLF/CR/LF terminators. Unicode separators are\n    contents, and a trailing terminator does not create a phantom line.\n    \"\"\"\n    selected = []\n    offset = 0\n    number = 1\n    for match in re.finditer(r\"\\r\\n|\\r|\\n\", text):\n        if start <= number <= end:\n            selected.append(text[offset:match.end()])\n        offset = match.end()\n        number += 1\n        if number > end:\n            return \"\".join(selected)\n    if offset < len(text) and start <= number <= end:\n        selected.append(text[offset:])\n    return \"\".join(selected)\n\"\"\"Acquire complete named-file bytes without newline translation.\"\"\"\n\nimport os\n\nfrom .core import TextStats, count_text\n\n\ndef count_file(path: str | os.PathLike[str], *, strip_bom: bool = True) -> TextStats:\n    \"\"\"Count a UTF-8 file silently, leaving its bytes unchanged.\n\n    Args:\n        path: String or filesystem path to the named input.\n        strip_bom: Remove exactly one leading BOM before counting.\n\n    Returns:\n        Immutable line/word counts using count_text's CR/LF and Unicode rules.\n\n    Raises:\n        OSError: The file cannot be opened or read.\n        UnicodeDecodeError: Complete input is not valid UTF-8.\n\n    The owned binary handle closes before counting; no caller stream is owned.\n    \"\"\"\n    return count_text(_read_text(path), strip_bom=strip_bom)\n\n\ndef _read_text(path: str | os.PathLike[str]) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Decode complete named-file bytes strictly, closing the owned handle.\"\"\"\n    with open(path, \"rb\") as source:\n        return source.read().decode(\"utf-8\")\n\"\"\"Public interfaces for whole-input text statistics.\"\"\"\n\nfrom .core import TextStats, count_text\nfrom .io import count_file\n\n__all__ = [\"TextStats\", \"count_text\", \"count_file\"]\n\"\"\"Expose the named-file/stdin command through python -m textstats.\"\"\"\n\nfrom .cli import main\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    raise SystemExit(main())\nPYTHON ?= python\nDIST_DIR ?= dist\n\n.PHONY: dist check\n\n# Explicit package/test files keep bytecode caches and workflow resources out.\ndist:\n\tmkdir -p \"$(DIST_DIR)\"\n\t$(PYTHON) -m tarfile -c \"$(DIST_DIR)/textstats.tar.gz\" textstats/*.py README.md Makefile AI_DISCLOSURE.md SDD-MANAGER.md docs tests/__init__.py tests/unit/*.py tests/integration/*.py\n\ncheck:\n\tPYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 $(PYTHON) -m unittest discover -s tests/unit -t . -v\n\tPYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 $(PYTHON) -m unittest discover -s tests/integration -t . Code inspection separately assessed all five production modules, all product tests, README/public guides, Makefile and ignore rules against S-1–S-4 and delivered S-7.\n\nThe pure core validates nonnegative integer results, strips exactly one initial BOM when selected, counts CRLF once before lone CR/LF normalization, preserves final-segment semantics and uses Unicode whitespace words. Facade exports and signatures are stable; acquisition and process state stay outside the core. Binary file reads preserve terminators and decode the complete input strictly. Context-managed owned handles close before result publication; read/decode/close failure propagates silently without a partial result. Real files plus injected permission/read/close seams establish portable failure/resource acceptance.\n\nThe command parser validates before acquisition and supports help, --keep-bom and -- dash paths. Only expected OSError/UnicodeDecodeError failures are translated to input-identifying stderr/status 1. Success emits one exact text line; invalid arguments retain status 2 and empty stdout. Cross-component tests compare actual module counts with the public API across BOM, Unicode and terminator inputs, and preserve bytes on success/failure.\n\nPublic docs distinguish delivered named-file behavior from planned JSON/stdin. Runnable examples match signatures, statuses and lifetime. contextlib\nimport dataclasses\nimport inspect\nimport io\nimport unittest\n\nimport textstats\n\n\nclass StatisticsTests(unittest.TestCase):\n    def test_public_statistics_value(self):\n 3))\n        self.assertEqual(stats, textstats.TextStats(2, 3))\n\n    def test_fields_are_immutable(self):\n        stats = textstats.TextStats(2, 3)\n        for field in (\"lines\", \"words\"):\n            with self.subTest(field=field):\n                with self.assertRaises(dataclasses.FrozenInstanceError):\n                    setattr(stats, field, 0)\n                with self.assertRaises(dataclasses.FrozenInstanceError):\n                    delattr(stats, field)\n\n    def test_negative_counts_are_rejected(self):\n        for lines, words in ((-1, 0), (0, -1), (-2, test_noninteger_counts_are_rejected(self):\n        for value in (1.5, \"2\", None, True):\n            for field in (\"lines\", \"words\"):\n                with self.subTest(value=value, field=field):\n                    counts = {\"lines\": 0, \"words\": 0, field: value}\n                    with self.assertRaises(TypeError):\n                        textstats.TextStats(**counts)\n\n    def test_zero_counts_are_valid(self):\n        self.assertEqual(textstats.TextStats(0, 0).lines, 0)\n        self.assertEqual(textstats.TextStats(0, 0).words, 0)\n\n\nclass TextCountingTests(unittest.TestCase):\n    def test_count_text_is_public(self):\n        self.assertTrue(hasattr(textstats, \"count_text\"), \"missing public count_text export\")\n\n    def test_specification_samples(self):\n        samples = (\n            (\"\", True, 0, 0),\n            (\"alpha beta\", True, 1, 2),\n            (\"alpha\\n\", True, 1, 1),\n            (\"\\n\", True, 1, 0),\n            (\"alpha\\r\\nbeta\\rgamma\\n\", True, 3, 3),\n            (\"alpha\\n\\n\", True, 2, 1),\n            (\" \\t\", True, 1, 0),\n            (\"alpha\\u2028beta\", True, 1, (\"a\\r\\nb\", 2), (\"a\\rb\\n\\r\\nc\", 4)):\n            with self.subTest(text=text):\n                self.assertEqual(textstats.count_text(text).lines, lines)\n\n    def test_unicode_whitespace_is_not_a_line_terminator(self):\n        for separator in (\"\\u0085\", \"\\u00a0\", \"\\u2003\", \"\\u2028\", \"\\u2029\", \"\\v\", \"\\f\"):\n            with self.subTest(separator=separator):\n                self.assertEqual(textstats.count_text(\"alpha\" + separator + \"beta\"),\n                                 textstats.TextStats(1, 2))\n\n    def test_only_one_initial_bom_is_removed(self):\n        for text, strip_bom, lines, words in (\n            (\"\\ufeff alpha\", True, 1, 1),\n            (\"\\ufeff alpha\", False, 1, 2),\n            (\"\\ufeff\\ufeff alpha\", True, 1, 2),\n            (\"alpha \\ufeff beta\", True, 1, 3),\n            (\"alpha \\ufeff beta\", False, 1, 3),\n            (\"\\ufeff\\n\", True, 1, 0),\n self.assertIs(parameter.default, True)\n        with self.assertRaises(TypeError):\n            textstats.count_text(\"alpha\", False)\n\n    def test_api_is_silent_and_input_unchanged(self):\n        text = \"\\ufeffalpha\\r\\nbeta\"\n        original = text\n        stdout, stderr = io.StringIO(), io.StringIO()\n        with contextlib.redirect_stdout(stdout), FileApiTests(unittest.TestCase):\n    def test_public_export_and_signature(self):\n        self.assertTrue(hasattr(textstats, \"count_file\"), \"count_file public export missing\")\n        signature = inspect.signature(textstats.count_file)\n        self.assertEqual(list(signature.parameters), [\"path\", \"strip_bom\"])\n        self.assertEqual(signature.parameters[\"strip_bom\"].kind, self.assertTrue(hasattr(textstats, \"count_file\"), \"count_file public export missing\")\n        cases = [(b\"\", (0, 0)), (b\"alpha beta\", (1, 2)),\n                 (b\"alpha\\r\\nbeta\\rgamma\\n\", (3, 3)), (b\"\\n\\n\", (2, 0)),\n                 (\"café\\u2028tea\".encode(), (1, 2)),\n                 (\"\\ufeff\".encode(), (0, 0)), (\"\\ufeff\\ufeff\".encode(), (1, 1))]\n        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as directory:\n            path = Path(directory) / \"input.txt\"\n            for data, counts in cases:\n FileFailureIntegrationTests(unittest.TestCase):\n    def test_real_missing_directory_and_malformed_files_are_silent_and_unchanged(self):\n        import contextlib\n        import io\n        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as directory:\n            root = Path(directory)\n            bad = root / \"bad.txt\"\n            original = b\"valid first line\\r\\n\\xef\\xbb\\xbf\\xff\"\n            bad.write_bytes(original)\n            for strip_bom in (True, False):\n                for path, exception in [(root / \"absent\", FileNotFoundError),\n       with contextlib.redirect_stdout(out), contextlib.redirect_stderr(err):\n                            with self.assertRaises(exception):\n                                textstats.count_file(path, strip_bom=strip_bom)\n                        self.assertEqual((out.getvalue(), err.getvalue()), (\"\", \"\"))\n                        self.assertEqual(bad.read_bytes(), original)\n"}
